# UltiTools-API — UAT Checklist

This document is the executable companion to `FEATURES.md`: one row per feature stating the
steps to exercise it and the observable truth that proves it works. It is an internal reference
for real-machine verification, not user-facing documentation.

> Batches are dispatched at 60 rows or fewer, and a batch never spans two repositories. There are
one standing exit to `human-uat-pending`: a row needing the pixel layer while the
> real-client harness is not ready. A row needing UltiCloud or panel credentials is run by the
> tester with the UAT account, not handed to the maintainer, and no row in this file uses Layer
> `human`. Beyond that, only a row the per-row triage assigns to the maintainer — because it needs
> the maintainer's own physical device or subjective human judgement, with that reason stated — may
> end `human-uat-pending`. Every other row must reach `pass`, `fail`, or `blocked`.

## Conventions

- **7.0 baseline:** executable server rows use Paper 26.3 build 258 on Java 25 and matching
  clients. Each framework version targets one latest Paper version, not an older-server range.
  Version-specific historical observations are not evidence that a row passed on this baseline;
  verify each Expected on the handed-over build. The retired diagnostic baseline below is historical only.
- **Columns:** `ID`, `Preconditions`, `Steps`, `Expected`, `Layer`, `Covers`.
- **ID:** cites its `FEATURES.md` ID verbatim. A negative case suffixes the checklist ID only,
  as `.neg-<slug>` — a negative case still tests the same feature, so the base ID is unchanged.
  The following existing probe IDs also keep their spelling; each maps to the named feature:
  - `ultitools.upm.list.click-buttons` → `ultitools.upm.list`.
  - `ultitools.upm.uninstall.{tasks-stop,no-jar,unreadable-entry,deferred,deferred-unrecorded,second-copy,cancels-staged-update}` → `ultitools.upm.uninstall`.
  - `ultitools.boot.deferred-removal.unreadable` → `ultitools.boot.deferred-removal`.
  - `ultitools.boot.module-api-invalid-missing` and `ultitools.boot.module-api-invalid-non-integer` → `ultitools.boot.module-api-invalid-refusal`.
  - `ultitools.config.conversion-skip-reason.multiline` → `ultitools.config.conversion-skip-reason`; `ultitools.config.reload-reports-unloadable-file.recovery` → `ultitools.config.reload-reports-unloadable-file`.
- **Layer**, using the real-client UAT vocabulary:
  `protocol`, `java-client`, `os-input`, `pixel`, `server`, `human`.
  Module fixtures (including named product modules) must be built for 7.0 and admitted on
  this baseline unless the row explicitly tests rejection. If a required fixture, service or
  observation channel is unavailable, record `blocked`; a substitute does not prove the row.
- **Unit regression controls:** `unit` marks repository tests, not real Paper UAT. A green unit row
  cannot discharge a `server` obligation; every configuration real-server obligation has its own row.
- **Delegated-authorization UltiCloud rows (D-27b, corrected by Phase 15 D-01, rewritten for protocol v2 in
  6.3.1):** a row whose Steps can only be exercised through an authenticated UltiCloud panel session carries the
  fixed Preconditions phrase `an authorized UltiCloud session under the maintainer's delegated authorization`
  (appended to, not replacing, the row's own preconditions) and Layer `server`. The maintainer delegates that
  authorization to the dispatching agent, which acts through the **staging test account** of the backend staging
  stack described below — never through the maintainer's own UltiCloud account, which no row logs in to, links or
  uses. A row whose Preconditions begin with the marker `` Env: `staging` `` (below) meets this convention through
  that marker and does not repeat the phrase. A row blocked because the staging stack or its test account is not
  available records that as its dispatch-time `blocked` status in the canonical verdict file, not as a property of
  this checklist's Layer column.
- **UltiCloud protocol v2 rows (6.3.1).** Every row of `## /ulticloud — cloud authentication` and
  `## UltiCloud connection — panel requests and delivery`, and every other row that reaches UltiCloud or its stub
  (the `/upm` stub rows, the panel capability rows, the log stream, metrics and remote surface guard rows, and the
  configuration rows that edit a file through the panel), begins its Preconditions with exactly one environment
  marker, and a row never mixes two environments. Each row also assumes `language: en` in
  `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` unless it says otherwise. The authority for which rows are such rows is the
  `CHECKLIST_IDS` list of `UltiTools-API`'s own automated check of them, `UltiCloudUatRowsTest`.
  - `` Env: `staging` `` — the backend staging stack (`https://api-staging.ultikits.com`, its gateway
    `wss://api-staging.ultikits.com/v1/agent` and its panel) and the **staging server root**, a Paper server
    running the staging build (handover item 1). The UltiCloud account is the **staging test account** the backend
    session provides for the run (its name starts with `uc-uat-`), never the maintainer's own account; every
    server it links is named `uc-uat-<run id>` on the confirmation page. Panel-side requests are sent by the backend
    session with its staging operations tool,
    `node scripts/staging-ops.mjs realtime <method> --server <server ID> --input '<JSON>'`. The tool and its `realtime` command exist on ulticloud commit `0b63cba` (branches
    `feature/p07-14` and `feature/p07-uat-triggers`), not on ulticloud `main`; the backend session runs them from a
    checkout of that commit with its staging ops token, which stays with that session — it is never copied into the
    run directory, a prompt, a log or a verdict file — and the run schedules its staging window with that session
    first. `<method>` is a Realtime RPC name of the tool's allow-list (`REALTIME_METHODS` in
    `scripts/staging-ops.mjs`), never a protocol frame type; the tool refuses any other name before it sends
    anything. The tool prints the response's `data`, which for `realtime` is
    `{"method":…,"serverId":…,"result":…}`, `result` being the RPC's return value
    (`apps/api/staging/ops/actions.ts`, `callRealtime`). A row names the method, its `--input` and the field of
    `result` it reads. In an input, `<C1>`, `<C2>`, … are fresh ULIDs (26 Crockford base-32 characters) the backend
    session generates, one per request, and `<RB>` is `{"kind":"system","label":"uc-uat-<run id>"}`. The RPCs that
    send to the plugin answer before the plugin does (`apps/realtime/src/rpc/contract.ts` at that commit):
    `enqueueCommand` and `requestPower` return `{"ok":true,"entry":{…}}` with the entry's `status` `delivered`, and
    the plugin's result is read afterwards with `listCommands` `{"limit":10}`, in the item whose `commandId` is the
    request's: its `status` becomes `succeeded` when the plugin's result is `ok` and `failed` otherwise, and its
    `output` is the plugin's `data.output` lines on success and `[]` on failure (`apps/realtime/src/hub/commands.ts`,
    `toEntry`), so a staging row observes only a command's success and its output lines. `requestOp` returns
    `{"ok":true,"opId":"<ULID>"}`, and the plugin's result is read with `getOp` `{"opId":"<that ULID>"}`, repeated
    while its `state` is `pending` (at most 30 seconds): `state` is then `succeeded`, `partial` or `failed`, `result`
    the plugin's data as the gateway maps it, and `error` the plugin's `{code, message}` on failure
    (`apps/realtime/src/hub/requests.ts`, `toLedger`). `requestModule` waits for the plugin and returns
    `{"ok":true,"outcome":…}`, the plugin's `module.response` payload unchanged. Gateway-side behaviour (the 10-minute expiry of queued
    commands, the 24-hour redelivery cap, a gateway-side `power_disabled`) is verified by the backend's own gateway
    tests, not by these rows.
  - `` Env: `stub` `` — the local stub gateway and catalogue (plan 22-19) and the **stub server root**, a second
    Paper server running the stub build (handover item 2). The stub server root never talks to staging and the
    staging server root never to the stub. Start the stub from a checkout of the handed-over commit, wrapping the
    Maven call in the machine's shared build lock: first
    `mvn -B -q -DskipTests test-compile dependency:build-classpath -Dmdep.outputFile=target/stub.classpath -Dmdep.includeScope=test`,
    then
    `java -cp "target/test-classes:target/classes:$(cat target/stub.classpath)" com.ultikits.ultitools.stub.UltiCloudStub --http-port 18080 --admin-port 18081 --access-log <run dir>/stub-access.log`.
    It prints `UltiCloudStub http  listening on 127.0.0.1:18080 …`, `UltiCloudStub admin listening on
    127.0.0.1:18081 …` and the access-log path; both ports bind 127.0.0.1 only. Stop it with `kill <pid>`
    (SIGTERM; a background job ignores Ctrl-C), which writes `STUB stopped` to the access log. The admin API
    takes JSON bodies on `http://127.0.0.1:18081`, and every POST writes one `ADMIN POST <path> <body> ->
    <status>` line: `GET /_stub` lists the endpoints; `POST /_stub/send` with `{"type":…,"payload":…}` (and
    optionally `"id"` or `"replyTo"`) sends one gateway-to-plugin frame built from its vendored fixture with the
    given payload keys replacing the fixture's, schema-checked first (an invalid frame is answered 400 and not
    sent; a missing `replyTo` names the newest request it answers); `GET /_stub/frames?type=<type>` returns the
    recorded frames the plugin sent (`&direction=out` the stub's own, `&direction=all` both); `GET /_stub/status` names the
    live connection and the credential fingerprints. A row names every other endpoint and body it posts. An
    access-log line reads `<ISO-8601 UTC instant> <CATEGORY> <text>`, with the categories `STUB`, `HTTP`,
    `HTTP-RESPONSE`, `V1`, `TCP-RESET`, `REST-VALID`, `REST-INVALID`, `UPGRADE`, `WS-OPEN`, `WS-IN`, `WS-OUT`,
    `WS-INVALID`, `WS-NOTE`, `WS-CLOSED`, `WS-ERROR`, `CREDENTIAL` and `ADMIN`; a credential appears only as
    `ucs_***#<first 8 hex of its SHA-256>`. The stub server root links to the stub with `/ulticloud login` from
    the console: the stub answers the first poll `authorization_pending` and approves at the second, so within about 15 seconds the console
    shows `UltiCloud: this server is now linked as <name> (server ID <ULID>).` and `UltiCloud: connected.`.
    "Linked to the stub" in a row means that has happened and `GET /_stub/status` shows one live connection;
    "connected" means `/ulticloud status` reads `Connection: connected`.
  - `` Env: `server` `` — no UltiCloud at all: the staging server root or the stub server root, used only through
    its console and its files; the row needs no network.
- **Handover items for the UltiCloud protocol v2 rows.** Each item is handed over with its path, version, byte
  size, SHA-256 and source commit; the SHA-256 is taken from a clean build after the final commit, and the
  executor fails closed on a mismatch.
  1. **Staging build** — the framework JAR built with `-Dultitools.api.url=https://api-staging.ultikits.com`.
  2. **Stub build** — the framework JAR of the same commit built with `-Dultitools.api.url=http://127.0.0.1:18080`;
     a separate JAR with its own SHA-256, never the staging build relabelled.
  3. **UltiLogin 2.1.0** — built against this framework (UltiLogin plans 01-02 and 01-03), for
     `ultitools.cloud.player-link` and `ultitools.cloud.player-link.redelivery-restart`.
  4. **UltiLogin 2.0.0** — the released JAR, for `ultitools.cloud.player-link.neg-ultilogin-2-0-0` only.
  5. **UAT probe module** — a test module built against this framework by the dispatching session (not part of
     this repository). Until it is in the handover, the rows that name it are `blocked`, not run. It has the
     identify-string `uatprobe`, `plugin.yml` `version: 1.0.0` and `api-version: 700`, and its enable hook:
     registers a panel responder under `uatprobe.echo` that answers the JSON object `{"echo": <params.text>}`
     (so its `hello` declares `modules:uatprobe@1.0.0`); registers the command name `uatsecret` with
     `UltiTools#getSensitiveCommandRegistry()`; registers a `@ConfigEntity("config/uatprobe.yml")` entity with two `@ConfigEntry` fields,
     the boolean `flag` and the string `label`, whose shipped file is the three lines `# uat probe settings`,
     `flag: false` and `label: probe`. Its
     commands, for any sender with no permission and no `requireOp`: `/uatsecret <text>` replies `uatsecret ok`;
     `/uatsecret fail <text>` throws `IllegalStateException` with the message
     `uatprobe failure on /uatsecret fail <text>`; `/uatprobe late` sends `uatprobe sync line` to its sender
     inside the command body and `uatprobe late line` to the same sender 40 ticks later; `/uatprobe late-secret`
     does the same with the later line `/uatsecret late-hunter2`; `/uatprobe console-only` replies
     `uatprobe console ok` when its sender is a `ConsoleCommandSender` and `uatprobe: run this command in the
     server console` to any other sender.
  6. **Credential relay** — a read-only helper supplied with the build by the dispatching session; given the server root, `<ID>` and the run id, it reports facts about `<server root>/.ultikits/credentials.json` as key names, JSON types and boolean check results only, never secret values or credential contents, and never modifies the file. For `cloud_credential.userId` it prints exactly `cloud_credential.userId.json_type: <type>` only when that key exists, where `<type>` is its JSON (RFC 8259) type name: one of `number`, `string`, `boolean`, `null`, `object`, `array` (not a host-language type such as `int` or `float`).
- **Catalogue rows without an environment marker.** A `/upm` row without an `Env:` marker that reads the
  catalogue uses the catalogue at the handed-over build's `api-url`, through the protocol v2 routes `/v1/catalogue/…`
  only (`ultitools.upm.update-check.v2`). Use the staging build (handover item 1) and the
  staging catalogue (`https://api-staging.ultikits.com/v1/catalogue/`). Catalogue requests carry no credential, so
  these rows need no linked server; before the window the backend session confirms that the catalogue holds the
  modules and versions each row's Preconditions name, and a row whose catalogue state cannot be arranged is
  `blocked`, not run. The API level these rows quote is this build's, written `L`: the build's version without its dots
  and without `-SNAPSHOT` (`7.0.0-SNAPSHOT` → 700). The build accepts modules that declare `api-version`
  700 to L (`ModuleApiLevels#MIN_INSTALLABLE_API_LEVEL` and `#frameworkLevel`). Substitute L in Preconditions, Steps and Expected before
  running a row.
- **Expected** must name an observable truth — an exact chat line, a log line, a database row,
  an inventory slot — and never the words "it works".
- **Covers** back-references a Phase 9 GUI-excluded class name; left blank when no such class
  applies.
- A row whose Preconditions name a prior row must appear after that row in file order — asserted
  mechanically: for every row, every checklist ID cited in its Preconditions cell must have a
  strictly smaller line number in this file than the row citing it (sweep class 8, D-27a).
- **Config-per-file rule (D-06):** one checklist row per `@ConfigEntity`-annotated class or per
  shipped yml file, never one row per key. This is the one deliberate exception to the "ID cites
  its `FEATURES.md` ID verbatim" rule above: a config-per-file row (ID suffixed `-yml`, e.g.
  `ultitools.config.config-yml`) aggregates every per-key `ultitools.config.<file-stem>.*` row for
  that file rather than citing a single one of them — there is no single per-key ID to cite when
  the row's job is exercising the whole file at once. A build-time (Maven-filtered) property —
  one resolved by Maven resource filtering at package time, such as `env.yml`'s `api-url` — is
  exercised by the build that produced the handed-over jar, not by editing the shipped file after
  the fact; such a row must state which build property it proves, and its Preconditions must
  state that the file it reads comes from a fresh, unmodified extraction of that build's jar at
  the property's shipped default, not a hand-edited copy.
- **Language for literal output.** A fresh 7.0 installation ships both `language` and `fallback-language`
  as `auto`. Unless the row explicitly tests a different locale or the unedited default, set
  `language: en` and `fallback-language: auto` in `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` before boot
  (or use a full `/ul reload` where the row allows it). Players use English and the console inherits it.
  follow its explicit locale settings instead. Unless a row says otherwise, compare the literal payload
  character for character, ignoring Paper timestamps, log levels and the Paper plugin-logger prefix.

## Command engine — 7.0

These rows need the disposable `cmd1-probe` module, not the separate UltiCloud `uatprobe` above.
Use only a disposable Paper 26.3 build 258 server on Java 25 with matched clients. Hand over
both JARs with version, byte size, SHA-256 and source commit. Record each required clause
separately; an unobserved clause cannot pass. No row authorizes release or a production action.

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.command-engine.typed | Probe loaded; two players `ProbeOp` and `ProbeUser` online; console access | From console run `cmdprobe count 5`, `cmdprobe echo 家`, `cmdprobe echo "a b"`, `cmdprobe say`, `cmdprobe say a b`, `cmdprobe give ProbeUser`, `cmdprobe slow` | Replies respectively contain `count=5`, `echo=家`, `echo=a b`, `say=[]`, `say=[a, b]`, `give=ProbeUser`; the slow reply starts `thread=` and identifies a non-primary command worker. No invocation throws a command error | server | |
| ultitools.command-engine.replies | Probe loaded; English framework language; operator client | Enter `/cmdprobe counnt x`, `/cmdprobe count abc`, `/cmdprobe count`, `/cmdprobe id extra`; inspect the input before submitting; submit each. Type `/cmdprobe ` and press Tab afterwards | Score each reply separately. The typo `counnt x` yields `Unknown command, please enter /cmdprobe help for help` then `cmdprobe help`. `count abc` reports the Brigadier integer parser error `Expected integer` rather than a usage line; `count` yields `Correct Usage: /cmdprobe count <count>` (verify the fixture maps the parameter as `count`), and `id extra` yields `Correct Usage: /cmdprobe id`. The four inputs can be submitted and valid literals still appear in Tab completion. Record the fallback argument hint separately. | java-client |  |
| ultitools.command-engine.literal-case | Probe loaded; console access; `ProbeUser` lacks `cmdprobe.secret` | Run `cmdprobe COUNT 5`, `cmdprobe AFTER MiXeD ADD` from console; as `ProbeUser` run `/cmdprobe SECRET` | Console replies `count=5` and `after=MiXeD` exactly once each; parameter case is retained. The unprivileged player never receives the `secret` body reply | server | |
| ultitools.command-engine.visibility | Probe loaded; `ProbeUser` non-op without `cmdprobe.secret`; `ProbeOp` op | Request `/cmdprobe ` completion as each player; run `/cmdprobe SECRET` as `ProbeUser`; run `/cmdprobe secret` as `ProbeOp` | `secret` and op-only `panel`, `replace`, `unload` are absent for `ProbeUser`; `secret` is present for `ProbeOp` and its body replies `secret`. The unprivileged error/usage does not list the hidden mapping | java-client | |
| ultitools.command-engine.unload-rebind | Probe loaded; op console; no other owner uses its roots | Record `cmdprobe id`, run `cmdprobe replace`, and record `cmdprobe id` again from console. Run `cmdprobe unload`, then request `/cmdprobe ` completion as the op client and retry `cmdprobe id` from console. Restart for cleanup. | Replacement reports `replacement=true`; the new identity differs and existing same-shape commands work without a commands event. After unload the root has no suggestions and cannot execute. Restart restores the disk-backed module | server |  |
| ultitools.command-engine.late-root | Disposable server stopped; probe config `extra: false` before this startup; then started with no `cmdextra` root | Confirm `cmdextra` is unavailable; edit the probe's `config/config.yml` to `extra: true`; run the real `cmdprobe replace`; try `cmdextra`; run console `minecraft:reload`; try `cmdextra` again | Actual replacement reports `replacement=true`; one warning names `/cmdextra` and says it becomes available after `/minecraft:reload` or restart. It stays unavailable before that event, then replies `extra` afterwards. `/ul reload` is not the new-root trigger | server | |
| ultitools.command-engine.hand-written | Probe loaded; op console; independent clean startup | Run `cmdraw ping` from console and request `/cmdraw ` completion as an op client. Run `cmdprobe unload`, then repeat console invocation and client completion. Restart for cleanup. | Before unload the reply is `pong` and `ping` is suggested. After unload the old child is neither executable nor suggested. The annotation-specific restrictions on raw trees are a source constraint, not inferred from this invocation. | server |  |
| ultitools.command-engine.lifecycle | Probe loaded with `extra: true`; op console; record `cmdprobe id` before the row | Run `ul reload` from console, then `cmdprobe id` and `cmdextra`; edit probe `extra` to `false`, run `ul reload` again and retry both commands. Restore `extra: true` and restart for cleanup. | The identity is unchanged after both configuration reloads and `cmdextra` still replies `extra`. For the bean conditional on `extra`, the diagnostic starts `[UltiTools-API] @ConditionalOnConfig drift after reload:` and ends `a restart is required to remove the component.` The configuration reload does not remove the registered tree. Do not substitute Bukkit plugin reload. | server |  |

## /ul — framework administration

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.ul.help | `language: en` in config.yml | Run `/ul help` (console or op player) | Chat/console output is exactly `=== UltiTools Commands ===` followed by `/ul reload Reload all modules`, `/ul reload <name> Reload a specific module`, `/ul list Check loaded module list`, `================` | server | |
| ultitools.ul.list | At least one KNOWN module confirmed successfully loaded (its name appears in the server's own startup log as enabled, not merely a JAR present in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` — an invalid module, or one excluded by `PluginDependencyResolver` for a missing or circular hard dependency, leaves `PluginManager#getPluginList()` without an entry despite the JAR being present) | Run `/ul list` | One line per loaded module reading `<module name> <version>`, matching `UltiToolsPlugin#getPluginName` and `#getVersion` for every module actually present, INCLUDING a line for the known successfully-loaded module named in the precondition — a zero-line or module-missing result is a fail, not a vacuous pass | server | |
| ultitools.ul.reload | Same precondition as ultitools.ul.list (appears after it), PLUS the UltiChat module specifically loaded and successfully enabled (the generic "at least one KNOWN module" precondition it inherits is not enough here — this row's second fixture needs `config/chat.yml` and its `chat.format`-driven behaviour to exist at all, which only UltiChat's own JAR provides); two concrete, named fixtures, not merely "every loaded module" in the abstract — a partial reload that updates only one module cannot be distinguished from a full reload without at least two independent fixtures: (1) the framework's own `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` `language` key, observable via `/ul help` showing the localized-vs-English block — reloaded through `UltiTools#reloadPluginsAndReport()` calling `reloadConfig()` then `initializeTextFallback()`, NOT through `reloadSelf()`, since the framework is not itself a plugin module in `PluginManager`'s list; (2) UltiChat's `config/chat.yml` `chat.format` key, observable via a plain chat message (the same technique `ultichat.uchat.reload` uses for its own `/uchat reload` case) — reloaded through `PluginManager#reloadAllAndReport()` calling UltiChat's own `UltiToolsPlugin#reloadWithReport()`, a genuinely distinct code path from the framework's own reload above; this row is capped at proving these two named, differently-implemented fixtures both pick up their new values in one `/ul reload` pass, not literally all sixteen possible modules, and makes no claim beyond them; `fallback-language: auto`; the server MUST be running with `language: zh` (NOT `en`) immediately before this row's own edit, so flipping it to `en` is a genuine state change rather than a no-op that would let `/ul help` show the English block even if `reloadConfig()`/`initializeTextFallback()` never ran | Confirm `/ul help` currently shows the Chinese `=== UltiTools 命令列表 ===` block (proving the pre-edit state is genuinely non-English); edit both named fixtures directly on disk (`config.yml`'s `language` to `en`, `chat.yml`'s `chat.format` to a distinguishable value); record `/ul list` immediately before reload; as an op player run `/ul reload` once; then run `/ul help` and send a chat message to surface each fixture's new value | Before the edit, `/ul help` shows the Chinese block, not the English one; the framework's own `reloadConfig()`/`initializeTextFallback()` pass and UltiChat's own `reloadWithReport()` both run with no exception in the console; a module list taken with `/ul list` immediately after is unchanged; `/ul help` NOW shows the new English block (a genuine change from the confirmed pre-edit Chinese state, not a vacuous match) AND the sent chat message is formatted with the new `chat.format` value — either fixture failing to pick up its new value is rejected, since it cannot distinguish "every module reloaded" from "only one did"; the reply in op-player chat is exactly one line, `All <n> modules reloaded.`, where `<n>` is the number of modules the `/ul list` above showed (English, because the framework's language is re-read before the modules reload) | server | |
| ultitools.ul.reload-module | English replies; rebuilt UltiChat with observable `chat.format`, as in `ultitools.ul.reload` above. Use the runtime name `<name>` shown by `/ul list` and confirm the old format with a test message. | Change `chat.format` on disk; run `/ul reload <name>`, then send a test message. Separately run `/ul reload does-not-exist`. Restore the original format and reload the fixture. | The named-module reply is `Module <name> has been reloaded` (green) and chat uses the new format. The missing-name reply is `does-not-exist is not a loaded UltiTools module. Use /ul list to see loaded modules` (red). | server |  |
| ultitools.ul.reload-log-line | `language: en` in config.yml (the Expected column asserts the literal English catalogue value); `fallback-language: en`; no override for `reload.module_done`; same module precondition as ultitools.ul.list (appears after it); at least two modules currently loaded, with their names and count recorded from a fresh `/ul list` run immediately before this row's own steps | Run `/ul reload` from the server console and capture the full console output produced during that single command | The console output contains exactly one line per module recorded from the preceding `/ul list`, each reading, verbatim, `Module '<module name>' reloaded.` (`reload.module_done` in `src/main/resources/messages/en.yml`) — every recorded module's name appears in exactly one such line, no module is missing, and no module's name appears twice; the module's own log output (if any) is a separate matter this row makes no claim about | server | |
| ultitools.ul.reload-language-pending | `fallback-language: auto`; the server running with `language: en` (started, or fully reloaded with `/ul reload`, after that value was set); UltiCleaner loaded and enabled (its name in the startup log); an op player online; record the loaded module count `<n>` from a fresh `/ul list` | Edit `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` on disk, setting `language` to `zh`, and do not run a full reload. As the op player run `/ul reload UltiCleaner`, then `/ul help`. Then run `/ul reload` and `/ul help` again. After the full `/ul reload` the reply is `已重载全部 <n> 个模块。` (the framework now speaks `zh`) and `/ul help` shows the Chinese block. ## /upm — plugin management

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.upm.check | `language: en` in config.yml; the startup update scan (triggered automatically at boot; watch the console until a terminal update-available/all-up-to-date line appears before running this row, rather than reading output at a fixed tick count) has already completed — `checkUpdates` only reads `UpdateManager`'s already-populated collections, it never itself queries or awaits the endpoint, as of 6.3.2, running this before that scan finishes instead replies `Update check still running; try again in a few seconds.`; at least one module update available — this is a deliberately-installed state, not the shipped default: install an older version of a module (not the framework — `PluginInstallUtils#installPlugin` only downloads into the module folder `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`, never the framework's own plugin JAR, so this recipe cannot produce a framework downgrade) with `/upm install <plugin> <version>`, choosing a `<plugin>` that is NOT already installed (no JAR for it in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`; if one is there, remove it with the server stopped before this install) — `installPlugin` only adds the requested version's JAR and never moves or deletes an existing one, so an already-installed module would leave two JARs and make the reported `<current>` version unreliable — using the local update-family fixture described in the fixture note `18-FIXTURE-update-family.md` — the public catalogue holds only one version of most modules, so it cannot supply the older version (#452); the older-version install alone does NOT reach this row's positive state — `UpdateManager#checkModuleUpdates` populates `moduleUpdates` ONLY during the one-time boot scan (`checkUpdatesSync`, called once from `UltiTools#onEnable`'s startup task); `/upm check` (`PluginInstallCommands`) never itself queries the endpoint or re-compares versions on demand, it only reads those already-populated fields via `getFrameworkUpdate()`/`getModuleUpdates()`. So after installing the older fixture, RESTART THE SERVER (a fresh boot scan) and again watch the console until a terminal update-available/all-up-to-date line appears before running this row. This row's precondition is established entirely by its own fixture install and restart — it does not depend on any other row's state or ordering. Within a batch, dispatch this row AFTER that older-version install AND its required restart | Run `/upm check` | Console/chat shows `Available updates:` followed by one `<name> <current> → <latest>` line per available update — `checkUpdates` formats the transition with a Unicode arrow `→`, not the ASCII sequence `->` | server | |
| ultitools.upm.check.neg-none | `language: en` in config.yml; the startup update scan (triggered automatically at boot; watch the console until a terminal line appears, as above) has already completed and ended with `[UltiTools-API] All plugins are up to date!` (as of 6.3.0 printed only when every lookup was answered; a failed lookup ends the scan with `Update check incomplete: …` instead, and `/upm check` then repeats that sentence rather than the line below); no update available for any module or the framework — this IS the batch's default install state: within a batch, dispatch this row BEFORE the positive sibling row immediately above installs its own older-version fixture, since that install makes this row's own precondition false until the module or framework is updated back to latest | Run `/upm check` | Chat/console line reads `No updates available.` (green) | protocol | |

## UltiCloud connection — panel requests and delivery

For the 6.5.0 command-boundary rows below, every command request uses a fresh envelope id and all ack/result assertions are correlated by `replyTo`, never by whichever frame happens to be newest. Stop, edit and start the disposable server whenever changing `ultipanel.commands.blocklist`; `/ul reload` does not reload that list. The list guards resolved outer spellings and synchronous nested framework commands, not vanilla/Bukkit targets inside `execute ... run` / `return run`, `commands.yml` aliases, datapack functions, third-party re-dispatchers or later-tick command blocks. Restrict those paths with `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: false`; they are not promises of this checklist's blocklist rows.

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.cloud.outbox.persist-restart | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.player-events: true` (shipped default); the stub server root linked to the stub and connected; a game player `P1`, offline | 1. `POST /_stub/unreachable {"on":true}` and wait for the console's first `retrying in` line. 2. `P1` joins, then leaves. 3. Stop the server; run `stat -c '%a' .ultikits/cloud-outbox.json` and read its `serverId` and, for each entry of `events`, its `id`, `type` and `payload.playerName`. 4. Start the server and wait for the first `retrying in` line; `POST /_stub/unreachable {"on":false}`; wait for `UltiCloud: connected.`. 5. `GET /_stub/frames?type=player.join` and `GET /_stub/frames?type=player.leave`; read the access log's `WS-IN` and `WS-OUT … ack` lines. 6. 35 seconds later read the file's `events` again | Step 3: the mode is `600`; `serverId` is this server's linked server ID; `events` holds one `player.join` and one `player.leave` with `playerName` `P1`. Step 5: exactly one `player.join` and one `player.leave` frame for `P1`, sent on the connection opened after the restart, carrying the envelope `id`s of step 3; their `WS-IN` lines are not marked `duplicate`, and a `WS-OUT … ack` line follows them. Step 6: `events` is empty | server | |
| ultitools.cloud.outbox.overflow | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; the stub server root linked to the stub, then stopped; its linked server ID `<ID>` (`cloud_credential.serverId`); a game player `P1`, offline | 1. Seed the outbox from the framework checkout the stub runs from: `java -cp "target/test-classes:target/classes:$(cat target/stub.classpath)" com.ultikits.ultitools.utils.OutboxSeedTool --server-root <stub server root> --server-id <ID> --count 1000 --force`, and record the first event's `id` in `.ultikits/cloud-outbox.json` as `<OLDEST>`. 2. `POST /_stub/ack {"enabled":false}`. 3. Start the server; wait for the `UPGRADE … -> 101` line and the 1000 resent `player.join` lines. 4. `P1` joins. 5. `POST /_stub/close {"code":1000}` and wait for the second `UPGRADE … -> 101` line and `UltiCloud: connected.`. 6. `GET /_stub/frames?type=outbox.overflow` and `GET /_stub/frames?type=player.join`. 7. Cleanup: `POST /_stub/ack {"enabled":true}`, then `P1` leaves | Step 1: the tool prints `OutboxSeedTool: wrote 1000 queued player.join events for server <ID> to <dir>/.ultikits/cloud-outbox.json (owner-only).`. Step 6: exactly one `outbox.overflow` frame, sent on the connection opened in step 5 after its `welcome`, with `payload.dropped` = `1`; the access log holds one `outbox.overflow` line. Among the `player.join` frames of the connection opened in step 5 (the `conn` number of its `WS-OPEN` line) there are 1000, one of them `P1`'s and none with the `id` `<OLDEST>`: the oldest event was the one dropped | server | |

Read the console and the newest line of `plugins/UltiTools/security/action.log.0`. 4. 30 seconds later run `/list` from the console and `GET /_stub/status` | Step 2: one `command.ack` with `replyTo` = the request's `id`, then one `command.result` with that `replyTo` and the payload `{"status":"error","code":"command_blocked","message":"command 'stop' is on the remote command blocklist — Blocked by policy — edit 'ultipanel.commands.blocklist' in plugins/UltiTools/config.yml to change this.","details":{"matched":"stop"}}`. Step 3: one WARNING `[Remote command] Blocked: stop`; the action-log line has `"capability":"COMMANDS"`, `"action":"execute_command"`, `"target":"stop"`, `"actor":"panel"` and `"verdict":"DENIED"`. copy the file aside. 6. `config.update` `{"path":"<P>","baseVersion":"<CV>","changes":[{"key":"label","value":"probe-staging"}]}`; `diff` the file with the copy | Every `getOp` ends with `state` `succeeded` and `error` null, and its `result` reads: step 1, `entries` include one with `name` `uat-rw.txt` and `type` `file`; step 2, `content` `uat rw 1\n`, `version` `<V1>`, `sizeBytes` 9; step 3, `version` equal to the file's new `sha256sum`, and the file holds exactly `uat rw 2` and a newline; step 4, `plugins` has an item whose `files` include one named `uatprobe.yml`; step 5, `entries` include the keys `flag` and `label`; step 6, `outcome` `saved` and `keys` `[{"key":"label","ok":true,"message":null}]`, and `diff` shows only the `label:` line changed, to `probe-staging` | server | |
| ultitools.cloud.files.neg-path-not-allowed | Env: `staging`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.file-read: true` and `ultipanel.capabilities.file-write: true`; editable roots at their shipped default; a directory outside the server root holding `outside.txt`, and a symbolic link `plugins/uat-escape` pointing to that directory; record the `sha256sum` of `.ultikits/credentials.json` as `<Hc>` and of `outside.txt` as `<Ho>`; this server linked to staging and connected, as for `ultitools.cloud.command.ack-result-output` (earlier in this section) | The backend session sends, one at a time, `realtime requestOp --server <server ID> --input '{"kind":<kind>,"payload":<payload>,"requestedBy":<RB>}'` with the `kind` and `payload` named below, reads the printed `result.opId` and then runs `realtime getOp --server <server ID> --input '{"opId":"<that opId>"}'` until its `state` is not `pending`: 1. `files.read` `{"path":".ultikits/credentials.json"}`. 2. `files.write` `{"path":".ultikits/credentials.json","content":"x","baseVersion":"<64 zeros>"}`. 3. `files.read` `{"path":"plugins/uat-escape/outside.txt"}`. 4. `files.write` `{"path":"plugins/uat-escape/outside.txt","content":"x","baseVersion":"<Ho>"}`. 5. Run `sha256sum` on both files | Every `getOp` ends with `state` `failed` and `result` null. Steps 1 and 2: `error` is `{"code":"path_not_allowed","message":"Blocked — 'credentials.json' is a protected credential file. This restriction cannot be changed through configuration."}`. Steps 3 and 4: `error.code` `path_not_allowed` and `error.message` beginning `'plugins/uat-escape/outside.txt' resolves outside the editable roots`. Step 5: `<Hc>` and `<Ho>` unchanged: no byte was read out or written | server | |

| ultitools.boot.deferred-removal | `language: en` in config.yml; ultitools.upm.uninstall.deferred (appears earlier in this file) has just passed; the server has then been stopped and `chmod u+w plugins/UltiTools/plugins` run | Start the server and read the start-up log; list `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` and `.ultikits/upm-transactions/`; run `/ul list` | Before module loading, INFO reads `Uninstalled module <plugin>: deleted <jar>.` The exact fixture JAR and its removal record are gone, and `/ul list` does not list the fixture. | server |  |
| ultitools.boot.deferred-removal.unreadable | `language: en` in config.yml; a POSIX host; the server process does not run as root (`ps -o user= -p <server pid>` does not print `root`; file permissions are not enforced for root); ultitools.upm.uninstall.deferred (appears earlier in this file) has passed again from a fresh fixture state, so `.ultikits/upm-transactions/` holds one `remove-*.json` and the module's JAR `<jar>` is still in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`; a copy of that JAR kept outside the server folder; the server has then been stopped and `chmod u+w plugins/UltiTools/plugins` run | 1. Run `chmod a-r plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<jar>` and confirm, as the server's user, that `cat plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<jar> > /dev/null` fails with `Permission denied` (if it succeeds, restore the permission and record `blocked`); run `sha256sum .ultikits/upm-transactions/remove-*.json` and note the value as `<record-hash>`. 2. Start the server and read the start-up log; list `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` and `.ultikits/upm-transactions/`; run `sha256sum .ultikits/upm-transactions/remove-*.json` again. 3. Stop the server, run `chmod u+r plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<jar>`, start the server and read the start-up log; list both folders again. Cleanup: stop the server, copy the kept copy of the module's JAR back into `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`, start the server | Step 2: SEVERE reads `Could not read <absolute modules folder>/<jar> while uninstalling module <plugin> (<error>) to confirm its identity; it was not deleted, and the next start will retry.` The JAR and its removal record remain and the record still hashes to `<record-hash>`. No removal-success or changed-file warning names it. Step 3: INFO reads `Uninstalled module <plugin>: deleted <jar>.`; both JAR and record are gone. A module that only lists the refused module under `softDepends`, `loadBefore` or `plugin.yml` `loadAfter` is not excluded (optional dependencies have a separate probe) | server | |
| ultitools.boot.plugin-load-order.neg-circular | Two installed modules declare a hard `@PluginDependency` on each other, and no other installed module depends on either; the server is NOT launched with `-Dultitools.useLegacyPluginLoading=true` | Restart the server and read the console output during startup | Console shows `[UltiTools-API] Circular dependency detected among plugins.` followed by a `Loop: A -> B -> A`-shaped line naming both modules; every module with no dependency path (direct or transitive) into the cycle still loads. Kahn's algorithm never assigns a node downstream of the cycle an in-degree of 0, so a module that depends on a cycle member is excluded too, not just the two modules forming the cycle itself | server | |

### Historical 6.x Paper-floor diagnostic baseline (retired for 7.0)

This is a historical record only, not an executable 7.0 acceptance baseline. The former
Paper 1.19.2 / 1.21.11 comparison and its pinned module bytes do not verify the Paper 26.3
release; none of the current rows above consumes these counts.

count or a supported-server claim for 7.0. The independent probe in
`ultitools.boot.skipped-handler-explanation` above verifies the retained diagnostic path.

#### Common to both servers

With these pinned UltiLogin bytes on a framework that no longer ships `SimpleHttpClient` and
`UltiCloudRequests` (removed in 6.4.0), both Paper 1.19.2 and Paper 1.21.11 print the following
records, with the same message text, count and order on each server. They are expected on both
servers and are not part of the 1.19.2 difference; the module still loads.

- C02 (WARNING): `[com.ultikits.ultitools.context.FinalContractValidator] cannot check @Final method contract for com.ultikits.plugins.login.service.PanelLinkTransport$Anonymous: com/ultikits/ultitools/utils/SimpleHttpClient$Response is not on the classpath`
- C03 (WARNING): `[com.ultikits.ultitools.context.FinalContractValidator] cannot check @Final method contract for com.ultikits.plugins.login.service.HelperPanelLinkTransport: com/ultikits/ultitools/utils/SimpleHttpClient$Response is not on the classpath`
- C04 (WARNING): `[com.ultikits.ultitools.context.FinalContractValidator] cannot check @Final method contract for com.ultikits.plugins.login.service.PanelLinkTransport: com/ultikits/ultitools/utils/SimpleHttpClient$Response is not on the classpath`

C02, C03 and C04 occur once each per server, and each is followed by a multi-line trace whose first
line is `java.lang.NoClassDefFoundError: com/ultikits/ultitools/utils/SimpleHttpClient$Response`; these
trace records are not additional WARNING messages.

## Economy

The five server rows use the same trigger: a verified UltiEssentials 7.0 build (`api-version: 700`) whose `DeathPunishListener` calls `EconomyUtils.isAvailable()`.
If that fixture is unavailable, mark the five rows blocked rather than assuming the old module JAR loads on 7.0.
Set `features.deathpunish.enabled: true` and `features.deathpunish.money.enabled: true` in the fixture’s `config/essentials.yml`.
Use a named non-op test player A without `ultiessentials.deathpunish.bypass`; record the permission check.
A’s world must not be listed in `features.deathpunish.world-whitelist`.
Run death steps as A; do not use an arbitrary player. For warning counts, first confirm startup produced no earlier warning naming the fixture.
The startup and unavailable-economy messages are English literals in both server languages; compare the log message body, not the Paper logger decoration.

This is the server's environment, not a defect in UltiTools or in 'UltiEssentials'. Install Vault and an economy plugin (e.g. EssentialsX) that registers a Vault economy provider to enable economy features.` | server | |

Install Vault and an economy plugin (e.g. EssentialsX) that registers a Vault economy provider to enable economy features.`; after the second death, NO further WARNING line for UltiEssentials appears — `EconomyUtils.reportEconomyStateIfUnavailable` warns each calling module at most once per server session (normal runtime; the public `EconomyUtils#reset()` explicitly clears deduplication and must not be invoked during the row), by design, not a defect | server | |
| ultitools.economy.attribute-caller | Repository unit controls for registration, external-plugin and package ownership; these are not inferred from operator commands or a historical module census. | Run the three cited attribution test classes and `EconomyUtilsReportingTest`. | Held by `EconomyUtilsExternalAttributionTest` (a connected external plugin is named; after it disconnects it is not), `EconomyUtilsRegisteringAttributionTest` (constructor, `@PostConstruct` and `registerSelf()` requests through the real `register(Class)` are named; a second thread started during registration and a request after a failed registration are not) and `EconomyUtilsOwnPackageAttributionTest` (a caller in the module's own package outside its declared root is named; two unattributable callers in different packages each get one warning) | unit | |

## Scheduled tasks

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.task.player-cache-sweep | A 7.0 probe registers an `@PlayerCache` bean implementing `ExpiringPlayerCache`, seeded with an expired entry and a still-valid entry, and an ordinary tracked bean seeded with an expired entry. Expose hook count and entry existence via a read-only probe command. Keep the seeded player offline. | Observe baseline state and handle count; observe the first scheduled pass after 6,000 server ticks and query the state again. | The opted-in hook count increases by one; its expired entry is gone and its still-valid entry remains. The ordinary bean still contains its entry. No `ConcurrentModificationException` or `Error running expiry sweep for <fully-qualified runtime class>` WARNING appears. Five minutes is nominal at 20 TPS, not a wall-clock deadline; absent probe/tick observation is blocked. | server | |
| ultitools.task.player-cache-sweep.neg-hook-throws | The preceding expiry probe plus a second opted-in bean whose hook throws before mutating its entry; both opted-in beans have expired entries and expose their state. | Observe the next scheduled pass and query both opted-in beans. | One WARNING for that throwing invocation has message body `Error running expiry sweep for <fully-qualified runtime class>` and carries its exception (same English literal in both locales). The retained entry was not mutated by this probe hook; the healthy bean ran and removed its own expired entry in the same pass. | server | |

## Module command and task runtime

The two reply rows use a disposable 7.0 probe instead of assuming a released module accepts non-player Brigadier senders.
Its synchronous `/fwreply` mapping accepts `CommandSender` with no player-only target and sends the literal `fw-reply`.
As of
6.3.1: `ultitools.runtime.panel-reply` (it read the v1 `command_result` text and the v1 log stream; the protocol v2
`command.result` carries the command's captured output, which `ultitools.cloud.command.ack-result-output` and
`ultitools.cloud.command.late-output` prove).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |

Do not use the old `enable-command-block` property as proof. | At a fresh position run `/setblock <x> <y> <z> minecraft:command_block{Command:"fwreply",TrackOutput:1b}`; place a redstone block at an adjacent position to power it once. Read `/data get block <x> <y> <z> LastOutput`. At a second fresh position repeat with `Command:"minecraft:list"` as the vanilla control. Restore any changed gamerule afterward. | The first fresh block records `fw-reply` in `LastOutput`. The second fresh block records vanilla’s list output; capture its actual 26.3 component/NBT representation rather than requiring the historical 1.21.11 rendering. A healthy vanilla control with missing probe output is a fail. | server | |

Restore the file from the backup, then set `announcements.bossbar.duration: 0` in that file (shipped value `10`; the field `bossBarDuration` accepts 1 to 60) and record the edited bytes. Run `/ul reload UltiTools-Chat` once from the native console and once as the op player, then `/ul reload` once from each sender; read each sender's reply (console output for the console, chat for the player) and the console log. Cleanup: restore the file from the backup and run `/ul reload UltiTools-Chat` | Step 1: each invocation writes exactly one partial-summary WARNING to the console, and the op player also receives the same text as a reply: `Module UltiTools-Chat reloaded partially; not reloaded: config-bound @Scheduled AnnouncementService.broadcastChat: AnnouncementConfig key 'announcements.chat.interval' has value 0 after the reload (a bound @Scheduled period or delay must be a whole number of seconds from 1 to 107374182 (0 does not mean off)); the running 300s is kept`; the console also shows the separate binding WARNING `UltiTools-Chat: AnnouncementService.broadcastChat is bound to AnnouncementConfig key 'announcements.chat.interval', which has value 0 after the reload; a bound @Scheduled period or delay must be a whole number of seconds from 1 to 107374182 (0 does not mean off); keeping 300s`. The file was not modified and the running values are kept - fix the value(s) and reload again.. See the console for details.` (the doubled period is part of the text), and the console log shows a SEVERE line `Module 'UltiTools-Chat' failed to reload: ` followed by the same reason; each `/ul reload` invocation replies `Failed to reload 1 of <N> modules: UltiTools-Chat. See the console for each failure.`, where `<N>` is the number of loaded modules. A plain success reply or a partial-reload reply to any Step 3 invocation is `fail`; the edited YAML bytes are unchanged by every Step 3 reload. Cleanup single-module reload replies `Module UltiTools-Chat has been reloaded` | server | |

By `PanelResponderOwnershipContextTest`: the call sees the receiving thread's context class loader (`responderSeesTheAdmittingThreadsContextClassLoader`) and no other thread-local (`arbitraryThreadLocalsAreNotCarried`); a queued request keeps the responder registered when it arrived (`queuedRequestKeepsTheAdmittedResponderWhenTheTypeIsReassigned`) | unit | |

This does not require a v1 FINE message or assert that starting shutdown drops a reply on a still-live channel. | unit | |

| ultitools.runtime.stop-log-clean | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.logs: true` (shipped default); the stub server root linked to the stub, so the console shows `UltiCloud: connected.` once per start (`AgentProtocol.java:387`) | Five times: start the server; as soon as the console shows `UltiCloud: connected.`, run `stop` in the console (within one second); after the JVM exits, search that run's `logs/latest.log` for `IllegalPluginAccessException` and for `[PanelMessageEvent] Failed to publish event`, and, after its `Disabling UltiTools` line, for `Failed to check`, `All plugins are up to date!` and `Update check incomplete` (the start-up update check, which starts at boot and is often still running when `stop` arrives) | In all five stop logs neither of the first two texts appears, none of the three update-check texts appears after `Disabling UltiTools`, and each stop completes normally (the JVM exits). Record for each run whether a terminal update-check line had appeared before `Disabling UltiTools`; a run where it had not is the one that exercised the update-check stop. The race is not forced on demand on a real server: a run with no observed panel frame during the stop does not exercise inbound shutdown handling; a run with no evidence the update check was still active does not exercise cancellation; record those unobserved obligations as blocked rather than pass, so all five runs are recorded with the time between `UltiCloud: connected.` and `Disabling UltiTools`. The deterministic halves are held by `ShutdownPanelMessageTracerTest` (every inbound type while disabling: nothing scheduled, one FINE line, no WARNING), the 6.3.1 shutdown window (every state-changing request after the disable starts answered with `The server is shutting down; this request was not carried out.` where a failure reply is defined; player-link completion is left for redelivery, nothing changed and nothing audited; read-only requests still served except `snapshot.request`, whose server-thread hand-off is dropped with no snapshots, result or action-log entry; an in-flight change finished before the action log closes, a handler that never ends delaying the stop by at most 5 seconds) by `ShutdownWindowTest`, `ShutdownPanelMessageTracerTest$Sweep#noNewRemoteActionIsAcceptedAfterDisableStarts`, `ShutdownGateTest`, `PanelChangeBarrierTest` and `ShutdownHandOffTest`, and the update-check half by `UpdateCheckShutdownAndOutcomeTest$Shutdown` and `UltiToolsDisableStopsUpdateCheckTest` | server | |

| ultitools.runtime.usage-lock-release | Repository unit regression for the conditions below; not a real-server verdict. | Run UsageLockReleaseOnRefusalTest and UsageLockReleaseOnThrowTest and inspect the cited assertions. | Held by `UsageLockReleaseOnRefusalTest`: after a cooldown refusal the lock is free (`cooldownRefusalReleasesTheLock`); after a parse failure and missing typed input the next valid call runs (`parseFailureReleasesTheLock`, `argumentCountRefusalReleasesTheLock`; the historical method names are retained, but 7.0 refuses these inputs before acquiring a validation lock); a refusal notifies only the validators that passed and runs no `onComplete` (`refusalNotifiesOnlyPassedValidatorsAndAppliesNoCooldown`); and by `UsageLockReleaseOnThrowTest`: a later validator that throws releases the lock (`throwingLaterValidatorReleasesTheLock`); a module parser throwing a runtime exception is refused before validators/locks, and the next valid dispatch runs (`throwingModuleParserReleasesTheLock`, `argumentParsingRefusalDoesNotEnterTheValidatorChain`), a later validator whose applicability check throws releases the lock and the original exception leaves with a failing refusal hook suppressed on it (`throwingApplicabilityCheckReleasesTheLock`, `throwingApplicabilityCheckKeepsTheOriginalException`), and an earlier validator's throwing hook does not skip the release (`throwingHookDoesNotSkipTheLockRelease`) | unit | |

## Module lifecycle registries

The supersede and post-load attribution rows are unit-pinned: only code calling `PluginManager#register(...)` with a newer
instance of a loaded module reaches the supersede path (measured, plan 17-79).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.lifecycle.supersede-releases-registrations | Repository unit regression for the conditions below; not a real-server verdict. | Run SupersedeRegistrationHandoverTracerTest and inspect the cited assertions. | Held by `SupersedeRegistrationHandoverTracerTest`: the newer copy loads, answers the panel type the older copy held and owns the completer, the older copy's subscription receives nothing, and all three were already released when the newer copy's `registerSelf()` ran (`newerCopyClaimsWhatTheOlderCopyHeld`); a newer copy whose `registerSelf()` returns `false` or throws leaves the older copy loaded, answering the type with its own responder, owning the completer and receiving events, with nothing of the failed copy left registered and a WARNING containing `M load failed` (a substring, not the whole message) (`newerCopyReturningFalseGivesTheRegistrationsBack`, `newerCopyThrowingGivesTheRegistrationsBack`) | unit | |
| ultitools.lifecycle.post-load-registration-attributed | Repository unit regression for the conditions below; not a real-server verdict. | Run PostLoadRegistrationAttributionTest and inspect the cited assertions. | Held by `PostLoadRegistrationAttributionTest`: a responder and a subscription registered by name from another thread after load leave with the superseded copy (`lateRegistrationsLeaveWithTheSupersededCopy`); during a supersede the loading thread's registration is the newer copy's and another thread's the older copy's (`duringASupersedeTheScopeDecidesAndOtherwiseTheOlderCopy`); with two copies listed it leaves with the first listed (`twoListedCopiesAttributeToTheFirstListed`); one copy's unload releases it (`oneLoadedCopyReleasesItsLateRegistrationOnUnload`); a name with no loaded copy stays name-only (`aNameWithNoLoadedCopyStaysNameOnly`) | unit | |
| ultitools.lifecycle.reload-keeps-registrations | Env: `stub`; linked and connected; `language: en`, `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: true`, no framework message overrides. Use the 7.0 probe with synchronous `/fwreply` (literal `fw-reply`) and a `/fwcomplete ` argument suggester returning `fw-option` for the English op test client. Record the loaded module roster and its count N before reload. Keep exactly one named op and one named second player online for the `list` control. | 1. From native console run `ul reload`, then `ul list`. 2. As the op invoke `/fwreply` and request suggestions for `/fwcomplete `. 3. Run `/ul help` as that player. 4. `POST /_stub/send {"type":"command.execute","payload":{"command":"list"}}`; record the returned `frame.id` and read the correlated `command.result` and console window. | 1. Console reply `All <N> modules reloaded.` and same module names as the baseline. 2. `fw-reply` and suggestion `fw-option`. 3. Block headed `=== UltiTools Commands ===`. 4. One INFO message body `[Remote command] > list` (Paper may prepend a logger prefix); correlated result is `ok` with `data.output` containing both online names, and the captured list output is not repeated in that console window. This row proves the specified command/suggestion paths, not independently the EventBus or responder registrations. | server | |

| ultitools.storage.transaction-failure-paths | Repository unit regression for the conditions below; not a real-server verdict. | Run TransactionFailurePathsTest and CommitOutcomeUnknownTest and inspect the cited assertions. | Held by `TransactionFailurePathsTest` (SQLite operator over an H2 file and MySQL operator over H2, both in MySQL mode): a rollback that fails once leaves the written row unstored, gives the caller its own exception, never switches auto-commit on and closes the connection (`failedRollbackDoesNotCommit`); under HikariCP nothing is stored, no connection stays active and the next transaction commits only its own row (`failedRollbackUnderAPoolLeavesNothing`); a commit injected to fail before it reaches the database stores nothing and is not followed by another commit (`failedCommitDoesNotCommitLater`); held by `CommitOutcomeUnknownTest` (the same two operators, and the acknowledgement case also under a HikariCP pool of one): a commit the database applied, whose acknowledgement then fails, leaves the row stored and raises the same `TRANSACTION_FAILED` `DataAccessException` (same type, error code, message and cause type) as a commit refused before the database, where the row is absent, and in both cases exactly one commit is issued, auto-commit is not switched on afterwards and the connection is discarded, so the outcome of a failed SQLite or MySQL commit is unknown to the caller (`commitAcknowledgementLostLeavesTheRowStored`, `commitAcknowledgementLostUnderAPoolLeavesTheRowStored`, `commitRefusedBeforeTheDatabaseLeavesNothing`, `sameFailureEitherWay`, `noRetryAndNoAutoCommitAfterAFailedCommit`); a rollback-only commit whose rollback fails stores nothing and throws `UnexpectedRollbackException` (`rollbackOnlyWithAFailedRollbackStoresNothing`); an `Error` rolls back, is rethrown as the same instance on both overloads, leaves no context, and the next transaction starts at depth 1 (`ErrorOnRelational`); a throwing rollback is suppressed on the original (`rollbackFailureIsSuppressed`); the JSON engine discards the transaction's staged writes on an `Error` (`ErrorOnJson`); a failed id backfill whose rollback fails stores no partial assignment (`StartupIdBackfill`); controls without injected failures and `@Transactional` with an `Error` on H2 behave as before (`Controls`) | unit | |

STARTSWITH `world_` neither reads nor deletes `worldX` and leaves the table definition unchanged (`startsWithUnderscoreIsLiteral`); `getLike` keeps its result (a pattern on the relational lanes, `world_`, `worldX` and `world_1`; literal on JSON, `world_` and `world_1`), `Query#like` `world_%` returns `world_`, `worldX` and `world_1` and EQUAL `world_` returns only `world_` on every lane, JSON included, and a null INCLUDE value throws `DataAccessException` (`unchangedOperatorsKeepTheirBehaviour`); INCLUDE `WORLD` matches the four lower-case `world` rows on real SQLite and real MySQL and none on H2 and JSON (`letterCaseIsTheBackendsOwn`). `RelationalOrderingContractTest`: `updateIf` with `level` GREATER 10 writes neither the row holding 2 nor the row holding 9, and `getAll` with `level` GREATER 9 returns only the row holding 10 (`updateIfHonoursNumericPrecondition`); eighteen GREATER and LESS conditions on `int`, `Long`, `double` and `Float` fields, over rows holding negatives, fractions, 9007199254740992, 9007199254740993 and 9223372036854775806, with thresholds including the `Float` boundaries LESS 0.1f, GREATER 1.1f, LESS 3.3f and GREATER 20.2f — eleven of the eighteen (`level` GREATER 9, GREATER 10 and LESS 0, `stamp` GREATER 9007199254740992 and LESS 9007199254740993, `ratio` GREATER 0.0, and `weight` LESS 0.0f plus the four `Float` boundaries) against a row holding that same value, so none of those eleven selects its boundary row, and GREATER 20.2f selects nothing under Java's typed compare — select exactly what Java's typed compare selects in `getAll`, `exist`, `page`, `del` and `updateIf` on real SQLite and both H2 lanes, and what a double-precision compare selects on real MySQL default columns (`getAllFollowsTheOracle`, `existFollowsTheOracle`, `pageFollowsTheOracle`, `delRemovesExactlyTheOracle`, `updateIfWritesExactlyTheOracle`); columns declared `BIGINT` and `DOUBLE` follow Java's typed compare on every lane, real MySQL included (`numericDeclaredColumnsUnchanged`); a String field still orders as text, EQUAL `level` 10 matches one row and EQUAL `stamp` 9007199254740993 matches one row (two on real MySQL text columns) (`unchangedOperatorsKeepTheirBehaviour`); a SQLite table created beforehand with TEXT columns keeps its `CREATE TABLE` text, ids and values, every numeric value stays `typeof` text, and `level` and `stamp` conditions follow Java's typed compare (`existingTextTableKeepsDefinitionAndCompares`); JSON with operator-assigned UUID ids selects what a double-precision compare selects (`jsonOrdersInDoublePrecision`); every numeric field has a condition a text comparison gets wrong, a `stamp` condition a double comparison gets wrong, and four `weight` conditions a 15-significant-digit reading of the stored float gets wrong, in both directions and one with no exact match (`everyFieldAndPrecisionIsExercised`); on real SQLite a `double` field written as 1/3.0 reads back as 0.333333333333333, `ratio` LESS 1/3.0 selects it, LESS and GREATER its read-back value select nothing, and `updateIf` with LESS its read-back value returns false (`doubleFieldComparesTheValueItReadsBack`). `RelationalExistenceCheckTest`: on real SQLite, `exist(WhereCondition...)`, `exist(entity)` and an `eq`-only `Query#exists()` return true for a stored match and false for none without throwing (`existCountsMatchingRowsOnRealSqlite`, `existByEntityOnRealSqlite`, `queryExistsOnRealSqlite`), as on H2 in MySQL mode (`existCountsMatchingRowsOnH2MysqlMode`, `existByEntityOnH2MysqlMode`, `queryExistsOnH2MysqlMode`). `SqliteFloatThresholdTest`, on real SQLite and `SQLiteDataOperator` over H2 in MySQL mode, each on a default and a `REAL`-declared `Float` column holding -2.5f, 0.0f, 0.1f, 1.1f, 3.3f, 20.2f, 2^60 and `Float.MAX_VALUE`: `del` with `weight` GREATER `BigDecimal` 1E+400 or `BigInteger` 10^400 leaves all eight rows (`delGreaterThanEveryFloatDeletesNothing`); `updateIf` with `weight` LESS `BigDecimal` 1E+400 applies to and writes all eight rows (`updateIfLessThanAnOutOfRangeNumberWrites`); `getAll` and `exist` with 21 thresholds of every finite number class (`Float`, `Double` values a `float` holds exactly — GREATER 0.5 and LESS -1.0 —, `Integer`, `Short`, `Byte`, `Long`, `BigDecimal`, `BigInteger`, out-of-range numbers, the `Float` boundaries) select exactly the rows an exact Java compare selects (`numberThresholdsFollowJava`); `weight` GREATER `Long` 2^60 selects only the `Float.MAX_VALUE` row and LESS a `BigDecimal` exactly equal to 0.1f selects only -2.5f and 0.0f (`valueEqualToStoredFloatIsNotSelected`); `weight` LESS `"abc"`, GREATER `"abc"`, LESS `""`, GREATER `"Infinity"`, LESS `"1e999"` and GREATER `"10"` select what `CAST(weight AS DOUBLE)` with the plainly bound text selects on the same database, or throw `DataAccessException` where that SQL fails (`textThresholdsSelectAsBefore`); on real SQLite, on both columns, `getAll` and `del` with GREATER or LESS `Float` or `Double` +Infinity, -Infinity or NaN throw `DataAccessException` and leave all eight rows (`nonFiniteThresholdsAreRefused`), `Double` thresholds 0.1, 1.1, ±1e300, `Double.MAX_VALUE` and `Double.MIN_VALUE` follow the exact Java compare (`finiteDoubleThresholdsFollowJava`), and GREATER `Math.nextDown((double) 0.1f)` does not select the 0.1f row on either column, the documented limitation (`seventeenDigitDoubleNextToStoredFloatComparesAsEqual`); on JSON, `getAll`, `exist`, `page`, `del` and `updateIf` with `Float` or `Double` +Infinity, -Infinity or NaN under GREATER, LESS or EQUAL throw `DataAccessException` and change no row (`nonFiniteValuesThrow`), and `updateIf` on an id that does not exist throws it too for each of those six values under each of the three comparisons, writing no row (`updateIfOnMissingRowRefusesANonFiniteValue`). `ConditionValueParityTest`, on real SQLite, `SQLiteDataOperator` and `MysqlDataOperator` over H2 in MySQL mode, real MySQL and JSON (as of v6.5.0, #815, #696): a `null` value under every comparison, on a text and a numeric column, throws `DataAccessException` with `DATA_ENTITY_INVALID` from `getAll`, `exist`, `page` and `del` and deletes nothing (`nullValueIsRefusedByReadsAndDelete`); `updateIf` refuses a `null` value under every comparison except EQUAL and writes nothing (`updateIfRefusesNullOutsideEqual`), while a `null` EQUAL value still writes a row whose column is unset (`updateIfNullEqualStillMeansIsNull`); `Double` and `Float` NaN, +Infinity and -Infinity under every comparison throw it from all five calls and change no row (`nonFiniteValueIsRefusedEverywhere`); the same calls with finite values answer normally (`finiteValueAnswers`); `del(WhereCondition.empty())` throws it and deletes nothing (`delRefusesAnEmptyCondition`); `exist(entity)` for an entity without an id returns false (`existOfAnEntityWithoutIdIsFalse`) | unit | |

## Opt-in module YAML files (6.4.0)

These server-layer rows require a disposable Paper 26.3 server on Java 25 and a purpose-built 7.0 probe module (`api-version: 700`).
explicitly selecting `CrashSafeYamlFiles`; no shipped module is silently migrated. The harness exposes console actions
`yamlfixture snapshot <name>`, `yamlfixture edit <name> <value>` (the saved snapshot, key `items` only), and
`yamlfixture create <name> <value>` (root map `items: <value>`), logging the returned enum or `IOException` class;
and `yamlfixture read <name>`, logging the harness’s live value. Keep live values consistent with the successful result only; constrain all names to the disposable directory.
An absent harness is blocked, not a pass. Use the default local POSIX filesystem with native rename/link support;
platform preconditions that cannot be observed are blocked, not synthetic Windows/ACL passes.

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.module-yaml.create | Harness above loaded; local POSIX parent exists; `new.yml` absent | 1. Run `yamlfixture create new complete`. 2. Read `new.yml`, record mode and list this target’s strict stage names in the parent. 3. Run `yamlfixture create new second`. 4. Compare bytes, mode and parent listing again | First result WRITTEN, complete map `items: complete`, mode 0600. Second result FILE_CHANGED and file remains byte-identical to step 2; no empty-then-filled final path is used (ordering pinned by the separate unit faults row), no temp remains on normal success | server | |
| ultitools.module-yaml.faults | Candidate checkout, JDK 25/Maven and default local POSIX filesystem; this is deterministic failure/process interruption coverage, not physical power-loss testing | Run `mvn -q test -Dtest=CrashSafeYamlFilesTest,StrictYamlPublicationTest,CrashSafeWriteGateTest`; inspect Surefire results, including child-process cases | All expected cases run and pass on the supported filesystem (check Surefire for skips, including parameterized and child-process cases). Short writes complete; injected write/metadata/force/close failures and AtomicMoveNotSupportedException leave old bytes without fallback; unsupported link creates nothing; appeared file/symlink/directory wins; temp ownership and error suppression hold; create order is completed/closed inode, link, directory force, temp unlink, directory force. Child halts leave old/absent or complete-new final bytes and no replay; strict/legacy concurrency and unchanged legacy maintenance are covered. A skipped platform lane is not evidence for that platform | unit | |
| ultitools.module-yaml.leftovers | Harness above loaded; server stopped; ordinary `old.yml` contains `items: old`; beside it plant `old.yml.ultitools-stage-0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef.tmp` with distinguishable complete YAML, and `old.yml.tmp-0123456789abcdef`; record hashes of both; do not target a ConfigEntity file | 1. Start server. 2. Read target and both leftovers. 3. Change target by hand to `items: operator`, run `yamlfixture snapshot old`, then `yamlfixture edit old new`. 4. Verify the target bytes and both leftover hashes, then stop/start again and compare all three | No startup promotion/replay or deletion: target stays old in step 2; later explicit opt-in edit returns WRITTEN, sets `items: new` and that value survives restart. Both planted files retain their exact hashes throughout. They are not inferred authoritative. Stop competing writers before manually inspecting/removing a recognized unneeded temp; protect it with the same care as the target | server | |
| ultitools.module-yaml.refuse | Harness on default local POSIX filesystem; ordinary `locked.yml` has `items: old`, mode 0400, owned and readable by the server user; `link.yml` is a symlink to an ordinary readable `referent.yml` with `items: old`. Record bytes, mode and link target; initialize both harness live values to `old` and verify them with `yamlfixture read` before editing. | 1. Snapshot `locked` and `link` while readable with `OperatorFiles.read`. 2. Edit each to `new` through the harness, then query both live values. 3. Create `link` through the harness. 4. Compare exact originals. 5. Restore fixture permissions. | The snapshot step can read the symlink’s referent; the strict publication step refuses it. Both edits return `REFUSED`, live values stay unchanged, and locked bytes/mode and link/referent are unchanged. Create returns `FILE_CHANGED` without changing the link. ACL/non-POSIX refusal and publication failures remain unit controls, not Windows or hidden-ACL claims. | server | |
| ultitools.module-yaml.write | Harness loaded; `edit.yml` contains `# handwritten`, `unknown: NOT_A_MATERIAL`, `items: old` in that order with consistent LF or CRLF; save exact original copy | 1. Snapshot edit, then edit to new through harness. 2. Compare entire file against original with only items changed. 3. Snapshot again, save a manual change to unknown, then edit to third through harness. 4. Compare file again and query the live value with `yamlfixture read edit` | Step 1 WRITTEN; step 2 every other byte, comment, unknown key and line ending matches. Step 3 FILE_CHANGED, no live-state update and manual bytes survive; items remains new, not third. No existing module, ConfigEntity or legacy OperatorFiles behavior is automatically opted in | server | |

## Module configuration persistence

Every row below that stops the server uses the console `stop` command and waits for the process to exit.
As of 6.3.0 nothing writes configuration at stop, module unload or module replacement (maintainer decision of
2026-10-04); a row proves it by comparing the file's `sha256sum` and `stat -c %Y` before and after the stop.
Killing the process, `/ul reload`, or a module's own reload command is a different path.
Later rows explicitly exercise other initialization, reload, panel, and interruption paths. "Disable line" means the console line
`[UltiTools] Disabling UltiTools v<version>` (Paper lifecycle output, not an en/zh catalogue key). It
The whole-map key, typed round-trip and semantic no-op feature IDs have no standalone Paper row; `ConfigDottedMapKeyTest`, `ConfigTypedCollectionTest`, `ConfigPresentInFileTest` and `ConfigEntityGoldenTest` cover the corresponding deterministic cases. The narrower real-server rows below are not substitutes for those complete contracts.
Retired for 6.3.1: `ultitools.config.panel-map-entry` (the protocol v2 `config.update` edits only typed entries and
refuses a map leaf per key, `RemoteConfigEntries.java:7-14,442-444`, which `ultitools.config.panel-map-entry-server`
proves on the stub; a 7.0-compatible fixture is required.

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.config.shutdown-keeps-operator-edit | 7.0-compatible UltiCleaner built against the handed-over framework (module `api-version: 700` at this baseline); verify from its source that `/clean status` and the quoted English/Chinese lines still exist, or block. In `plugins/UltiTools/pluginConfig/UltiCleaner/config/cleaner.yml`, with fresh `item.enabled: true`, use `item.interval: 300` as `<R>`. No module-side changes to that setting; run steps 1–3 before any cleanup fires. Pin the actual reply locale. | 1. From the console run `/clean status` and record the number `<S0>` from the line `Next item cleanup: N seconds` (N is the number; under `language: zh` the same line reads `下次物品清理: N 秒`). 2. With the server still running, change `item.interval` in `cleaner.yml` to `3000`, save the file, and record `sha256sum` of the file as `<H1>` and `stat -c %Y` as `<M1>`. 3. Run `/clean status` again and record `<S1>`. 4. Run `stop` and wait for the process to exit. 5. Record `sha256sum` of `cleaner.yml` as `<H2>`, `stat -c %Y` as `<M2>`, and read its `item.interval`. 6. Start the server and, within 60 seconds of the `Done` line, run `/clean status`; record `<S2>`. 7. Restore: set `item.interval` back to `<R>`, run `stop`, start the server, and confirm `/clean status` shows a number no greater than `<R>` | `<S0>` and `<S1>` are both no greater than `<R>` (the edit is not live before the restart, so the running value differs from the edited one); the stop console output contains the disable line and no WARNING line containing `cleaner.yml` (UltiCleaner changed nothing in memory, so nothing is named as never saved); `<H2>` equals `<H1>`, `<M2>` equals `<M1>` and `item.interval` still reads `3000` - nothing wrote the file at stop; `<S2>` is greater than `<R>` and no greater than `3000`. A `<H2>` different from `<H1>`, or `item.interval` back at `<R>`, is a fail (the pre-6.3.0 behaviour) | server | |
