## Configuration real-server obligations

Use a `ConfigFixture` rebuilt for the handed-over UltiTools 7.0 build (Java 25, module `api-version: 700` at this baseline). Its source, source head and artifact identity are required in the handoff;
the historical Phase 17 JAR and legacy catalogue do not qualify. Confirm the stated commands and values in the handed-over source; block any affected row if missing. Install it in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`. `F` below means
`plugins/UltiTools/pluginConfig/ConfigFixture/config/fixture.yml`. Record complete before/after
files and restore the per-row backup. Before the first row, start the server once with the fixture build under test
and stop it, so F holds every setting that build declares (a rebuilt fixture's first start appends any setting it
added, for example `# Map-valued entries` / `rules: {}` since plan 17-74); capture the baseline F only after that start.
A baseline captured before it makes every row that compares F across a start show those lines and give a wrong
verdict. Start from the positive fixture baseline for each row;
never carry a damaged file or ambiguous-map specimen into the next row. These server rows do not
count Maven tests as real-server evidence. No write normalizes layout: in every row, each byte of F outside what
the row's step writes stays as it was (compare with `diff` or `cmp`), and a file the write gate cannot write that way
is left unchanged with the applicable diagnostic; a line number is reported for layout refusals when available.

The fixture is the one plan 17-67 rebuilt: besides `capture`, `give`, `set <whole-key> <value>` (adds or changes
one `aliases` entry and calls `save()`), `show` and `saveloop <n>`, it has `setmem <value>` (changes `motto` in
memory only, reply `ConfigFixture motto in memory=<value> (not saved)`), `setmotto <value>` (changes `motto` and
calls `save()`, reply `ConfigFixture save() called with motto=<value>`; the reply does not report the write outcome -
F and the log are the evidence) and `opset <whole-key> <value>` (sets one `aliases` entry and writes exactly that
entry with `saveOperatorMapEntry`; reply `ConfigFixture operator change written: alias [<key>]=<value>`, or
`ConfigFixture operator change NOT saved: <reason>` with the in-memory change rolled back). Its baseline F has two
token entries, `enabled` and `mode`, whose framework comments are `# Fixture enabled (English token text).` and
`# Fixture mode (English token text).` under `language: en` (`# 测试配置启用（中文注释）。` and
`# 测试配置模式（中文注释）。` under `zh`); the rebuilt fixture must supply these keys in a 7.0 message catalogue rather than the removed legacy language files; `show` prints `mode=<value>` on its line that starts `ConfigFixture price=`.
Plan 17-76 added the settings `warn-times`, `lines`, `rate` and `limits`, the command `decl-check <unchecked|plain>`, and
a last `show` line `ConfigFixture validation warn-times=<list> lines=<n> rate=<value> limits=<n>`. "F0" below is a copy of
the baseline F taken before the row.

## Panel capabilities

As of 6.3.1 every row below runs on the stub (protocol v2): it sets the named `ultipanel.capabilities.*` key,
then sends the matching request with `POST /_stub/send` or reads the matching outbound frames. The switches decide
the capabilities the server declares in its `hello` (mapping in `AgentCapabilities`' class comment: `commands`;
`files:read` and `config:read` from `file-read`; `files:write` and `config:write` from `file-write`; `files:delete`;
`logs`; `telemetry` from `monitoring`; `events:players` and `events:commands` from `player-events`;
`properties:read` and `properties:write` from `server-properties`). Every inbound request is checked again against
its switch when it arrives (`AgentProtocol#inboundAllowed`), so a request whose switch is off is answered `error`
`capability_not_accepted`, with `details.capability` the contract capability and `message` the sentence
`Capability#refusalMessage()` produces (`Blocked by policy — edit 'ultipanel.capabilities.<key>' in
plugins/UltiTools/config.yml to change this.`), and it adds one DENIED line to
`plugins/UltiTools/security/action.log.0` whose `action` is the request type and whose `target` is the request's
`command` or `path`, or else its type (`CloudFeatures#recordSwitchRefusal`). A `/ul reload` that changes the set of
declared capabilities closes the connection normally and reconnects with a new `hello` (`UltiCloud: the reload
changed which capabilities this server offers; reconnecting so UltiCloud sees the new set.`,
`ultitools.cloud.capability-switch-off`), so `/ul reload` is enough for every switch. A row's Preconditions name the
state of its switch and the newest `hello` (`GET /_stub/frames?type=hello`) that shows it. The stub sends a request
whatever the `hello` declared and notes `WS-NOTE … <type> sent although its capability …` when it was not declared.
Retired for 6.3.1: `ultitools.capability.file-delete.recursive` and `.neg-recursive-unset` (the contract's
`files.delete` has no `recursive` field and a directory is never deleted, see `.neg-directory`), and
`ultitools.capability.server-properties.update-config` and `.neg-update-config-disabled` (they sent the v1
`update_config` with `fileName: server_properties`; protocol v2 reaches `server.properties` only through
`properties.get` and `properties.update`, whose switch the two `server-properties` rows check with `file-write` set
the other way).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Live log stream controls

As of 6.3.1 the log stream is protocol v2's `log.lines`: while `logs` is accepted the server sends the console's
lines every second (`CloudFeatures.java:103,265`), filtered by `ultipanel.logging.levels` and
`ultipanel.logging.excluded-loggers` as read when the connection's log capture is attached
(`AgentOutboundFeeds.java:342-353`, `SystemLogHandler#loadConfiguration`); a `log.lines` entry carries only `ts`,
`level` (`DEBUG`, `INFO`, `WARN` or `ERROR`) and `message`. Retired for 6.3.1: `ultitools.remote.log-stream-pause`,
`ultitools.remote.log-stream-levels`, `ultitools.remote.log-stream-levels-direct`,
`ultitools.remote.log-stream-batch-interval` (with `.neg-below-floor`), `ultitools.remote.log-stream-start-stop` and
`ultitools.remote.log-stream-status`: each sent the v1 `log_stream` message, and the protocol v2 contract has no panel
request that controls or queries the log stream. The unit-pinned rows below hold the v1 batch sender, which 6.3.1
keeps, unreachable, until 6.4.0 (22-DECISIONS S-67).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.remote.error-auto-report | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; the UAT probe module (handover item 5) loaded; `ultipanel.capabilities.logs: true` and `ultipanel.logging.error-reporting.enabled: true` (shipped defaults); `ultipanel.logging.levels: [info, warning]` added to config.yml (it leaves out `error`), the server restarted after the edit; the stub server root linked to the stub and connected, its newest `hello` declaring `logs` and `errors`; a game player `P1` in game | 1. `P1` runs `/uatsecret fail uat-err`. 2. Ten seconds later `GET /_stub/frames?type=log.lines` and `GET /_stub/frames?type=error.report`. 3. Cleanup: remove `ultipanel.logging.levels` and restart | Step 2: one `error.report` frame whose payload `message` is `uatprobe failure on /uatsecret ***`, while no `log.lines` entry on that connection has `level` `ERROR` and none contains `uatprobe failure`; `log.lines` entries at level `INFO` keep arriving (the control): the `levels` setting filters only the log stream, never error reporting (`SystemLogHandler.java:230-238,247-256`) | server | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-wording | If extracting from the built JAR: the jar under `target/` is a fresh build of the commit being verified, not a stale artifact from an earlier build in the same worktree. If reading source directly instead: checked out at this pull request's head | Extract `config.yml` from the built JAR (`unzip -p target/UltiTools-API-<version>.jar config.yml`) or read `src/main/resources/config.yml` directly; locate the comment lines immediately above `ultipanel.capabilities.logs: true` and the comment lines immediately above `ultipanel.logging.batch.interval: 5000`; separately read `Capability.LOGS`'s description strings in `src/main/java/com/ultikits/ultitools/entities/Capability.java`; separately read the comment lines immediately above `logs: true` in `config-example.yml` at the repository root | The `logs` comment in `config.yml` (English and Chinese), the `logs` comment in `config-example.yml` (English and Chinese), and `Capability.LOGS`'s compiled description all state that the switch decides whether the live log stream is delivered ("Whether the panel receives the live console log stream." / "面板是否可以接收实时控制台日志流。"), with no reference to the panel controlling, pausing or resuming the stream — none of the three contains the English phrase "stream and control" or the Chinese phrase "接收并控制"; the `ultipanel.logging.batch.*` comments in `config.yml` (English and Chinese) state that the three keys belong to the deprecated protocol v1 sender and that the UltiCloud protocol 2.1.0 session ignores them; neither language contains a "read once"/"once, at server start" ("只在服务器启动时读取一次") claim (#495 fixed) | protocol | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-held-batch | not covered — unit-pinned. A send failing between the connection check and the write (the socket closing while a batch is in flight) cannot be arranged on demand on a real server | — | Held by `LogBatchHoldTest`: on the transmitter's own sender a throwing send and a send into an already-closed socket keep the batch and send it first (`throwingSendIsHeldAndSentFirst`, `closedSocketSendIsHeldAndSentAfterReconnect`), and no newer record is drained while it is held (`noNewerRecordIsDrainedWhileHeld`); the monitor's `batch_update` drain does the same (`throwingExternalSendIsHeld`, `closedSocketExternalSendIsHeld`) | server | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-overflow-report | not covered — unit-pinned. With the panel connected the queue is drained each time it reaches the batch size, and a disconnected server does not queue at all, so an overflow cannot be produced on demand | — | Held by `LogQueueOverflowReportTest`: 1005 records into a full queue report `discarded 5 record(s)` once, a second report within the interval is suppressed, the next interval reports the new count (`overflowDiscardsAreCountedAndReportedOncePerInterval`); nothing discarded, nothing reported (`noDiscardNoReport`) | server | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-relog-safe | not covered — unit-pinned. The deadlock needs the socket to close between the drain thread's `isOpen()` check and `onClose`, while the main thread crosses the batch-size threshold at the same moment; neither can be arranged on demand on a real server | — | Held by `SysErrRelogDeadlockTest` (Paper's `SysoutCatcher` reproduced: a `System.err` line is re-logged through the plugin logger): the client's closed-socket diagnostic sent under the drain lock does not deadlock, reaches the console and is not streamed (`closedSocketDiagnosticUnderDrainLockDoesNotDeadlock`); any `System.err` line re-logged under the drain lock does not deadlock and is not streamed (`anyRelogUnderDrainLockDoesNotDeadlock`); control: the same line outside the drain lock is streamed (`relogWithoutDrainLockIsStillStreamed`). Both deadlock cases fail on `fd875537` | server | |

Restart the server. 2. After `UltiCloud: connected.`, wait ten seconds; read the access log's `WS-OPEN` line of the new connection and `GET /_stub/frames?type=log.lines`. 3. Control: read the server's `logs/latest.log` | Step 2: a `log.lines` frame on the new connection carries an entry whose `message` is `[UltiTools-API] Plugin load order resolved successfully.` (logged while modules load, `PluginManager.java:3675`) and whose `ts` is earlier than that connection's `WS-OPEN` time: the start-up records are kept and replayed to the first connection, each with the time it was logged (`AgentOutboundFeeds.java:342-353`, `EarlyLogCapture#drainInto`). Five seconds later `GET /_stub/frames?type=log.lines` | Step 5: entries whose `message` is the console line's text without colour codes and, for a plugin's own line, without the `[<plugin>] ` prefix Paper adds when it prints that line on the console: for step 1 the operator's quit line and join line; for step 2 `<operator> issued server command: /list` (the operator's own `list` reply goes to that operator only, so it is neither on the console nor in the stream); for step 3 the `say` broadcast line and `[Remote command] > say mirror-check`, the plugin line appearing exactly once although Paper also copies it into Log4j (before 6.3.0 none of these console lines except that plugin line reached the panel) | server | |

## Panel upload and metrics honesty

Retired for 6.3.1: `ultitools.remote.upload-config` and `.neg-permissions` (the protocol v2 contract has no
`upload_config` message; configuration files are edited with `config.update`) and `ultitools.remote.disk-usage` (the
v2 `status` frame has no disk-usage field, `AgentTelemetry.java:388-397`, so the panel no longer receives it).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.remote.enabled-plugins | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.monitoring: true` (shipped default); the stub server root linked to the stub and connected; a way to put one non-essential, already-loaded plugin `<target>` into the loaded-but-disabled state without removing it from `Bukkit.getPluginManager().getPlugins()` (for example `Bukkit.getPluginManager().disablePlugin(target)` from a console script), restored once the row is done | 1. `GET /_stub/frames?type=plugins.snapshot` and record the newest frame's `payload.plugins` as `<before>`. 2. Put `<target>` into the loaded-but-disabled state. 3. Twenty-five seconds later read the newest `plugins.snapshot` again | Step 3: the newest `plugins.snapshot` lists the same plugins as `<before>`, `<target>` included, and `<target>`'s entry now has `enabled` false while every other entry's `enabled` is unchanged (`AgentTelemetry.java:478-488`): the snapshot reports each plugin's real state, not every installed plugin as enabled (#437). A `plugins.snapshot` is sent at most once every 10 seconds when the list changed (`AgentTelemetry.java:57`) | server | |

## Remote surface guards

As of 6.3.1 every row below runs on the stub (protocol v2). A request path is a contract `ServerPath`, which never
holds a `.` or `..` segment, a leading `/`, a backslash or a colon: the stub answers such a `POST /_stub/send` 400
and sends nothing, so the framework's own handling of `..` after a symbolic link is reached by no protocol v2
request. A refused file request is answered `error` `path_not_allowed` with the guard's refusal sentence as
`message` (`RemoteFileOperations.java:667-673`), and a `files.list` leaves out every entry the guard would refuse
instead of marking it (`RemoteFileOperations.java:286-290`). Retired for 6.3.1:
`ultitools.remote.server-properties-safe-keys.neg-unsafe-key` (the contract's `properties.update` cannot name a key
outside its twelve, so no request reaches the framework; `ultitools.cloud.properties.update.neg-refused-key` proves
it for `online-mode` and `white-list`).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| ultitools.config.batch-buffering | Checkout at the refactor head; JDK 25 and Maven dependencies available | Run `mvn test -Dtest=ConfigBatchBufferingTest`; inspect its refused-new-files, refused-existing-bytes, validation-before-write, outer-package-refusal, accepted-write-failure, protected-input and standalone controls | Seven tests pass: refused batches create/change no file; validation observes no pending file; an accepted second-file I/O failure preserves the first successful write and original second bytes, protects the failed entity and writes later files; standalone registration writes immediately | unit | |

One row per shipped yml file (D-06's config-per-file rule), not per key: `config.yml` (52 shipped leaf keys)
and `env.yml` (two build-time keys, `api-url` and `version`). The runtime row confirms each shipped key and
default, then changes representative live settings. The `env.yml` row checks the unmodified artifact;
its build-time keys are not live server settings.

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.config.config-yml | Env: `stub`; fresh `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` from the build under test, unedited for the shipped-default check; disposable linked server, negotiated logs enabled, with remote commands accepted once enabled. Use a 7.0 UAT probe (handover item 5) and one OP player. Supply a Paper 26.3-compatible external-plugin lifecycle probe or supported enable/disable harness that invokes `UltiToolsAPI#connect`/`#disconnect`; otherwise the logger-exclusion clause is blocked. No framework text overrides. | 1. Confirm all 52 shipped leaf defaults, including `language: auto`, `fallback-language: auto` and both lists. 2. Set `language: en`, `fallback-language: en` and `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: true`; restart and run `ul help` at the console. 3. With only `ultipanel.logging.levels: ["error"]` added, restart, wait for `UltiCloud: connected.`, capture new `log.lines` from that link, remotely send `say levels-info` and have the player run `/uatsecret fail uat-err`. 4. Remove `levels`, set `ultipanel.logging.excluded-loggers: ["Minecraft"]`, restart, wait for the link, trigger the supplied external-plugin disconnect/connect and record matching console lines and new frames. Repeat step 4 with the exclusion removed and the same trigger; restore the original file. | 1. All 52 defaults match; opt-in filter keys are absent. 2. First line: `=== UltiTools Commands ===` (`ul.help`, en.yml:148; zh is `=== UltiTools 命令列表 ===`). 3. The INFO positive control occurs in the server console but does not stream; an `ERROR` entry containing `uatprobe failure` streams and no new `INFO` entry streams. 4. On each run the console has `[UltiTools-API] External plugin disconnected: <name>` and `[UltiTools-API] External plugin connected: <name> v<version>` (unlocalized, `UltiToolsAPI.java:90–91,115–116`); neither streams with exclusion, both stream with it removed. Confirm the stream is active in both runs using a non-Minecraft logger. The three legacy `batch.*` keys are resource-only checks. | protocol | |
| ultitools.config.env-yml | The jar handed over for this dispatch — a single clean build of the current commit at its default Maven properties, no `-Dultitools.api.url` override — is a fresh, unmodified extraction target, not a hand-edited copy; the `pom.xml` `<version>` at the built commit known in advance (read it before extracting). This is a build-time (Maven-filtered) property: it is exercised by the build that produced the handed-over jar, not by editing the shipped `env.yml` file after the fact, and this row proves only that default-property build | Unzip the handed-over jar and read `env.yml` | `env.yml` reads `api-url: "https://api.ultikits.com"` and `version: <the pom.xml version>` (`7.0.0-SNAPSHOT` at this baseline) — Maven's resource filtering resolved both placeholders, neither is the literal `${project.version}` token. This row proves only the default-property build; proving the `-Dultitools.api.url` override path requires a second, separately-built and separately-hashed jar handed over for that specific purpose — building one ad hoc during a dispatch is out of scope for this phase's boundary against constructing new product artifacts mid-run | protocol | |

## UltiCloud frozen contracts 2.1.0 / 1.1.0 (as of 6.3.1)

These four regression rows are prepared for independent UAT, not recorded as executed here. The coordinator
supplies a clean, separately hashed staging build of the source head, the staging window, the test account,
a read-only incoming-frame observer and the exact catalogue fixture identifiers in the run's handover.
The scope is backup-id validation and capability refusal, long-changelog integrity-checked installation,
complete version pagination and unchanged property edits; unrelated UI, authentication and load tests are
outside this batch. Missing prerequisites are `blocked`, never a substitute observation or a pass.

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.cloud.capability-switch-off.neg-backup-id | Env: `staging`; the handed-over framework build speaks protocol 2.1.0 and the staging API includes the boundary changes from UltiKits/ulticloud#313 and #315 and plan 20.1-02; the staging test account owns the linked, connected test server; no loaded module declares `backups`, and the current handshake confirms that it is not accepted; the read-only protocol observer records incoming gateway frame types, with the connection's `welcome` as its positive control | 1. Record the observer position. 2. In the authorized staging session send `DELETE …/backups/item?backupId=..` for that server, with the route's normal authorization and client headers. 3. Repeat with the valid identifier `uat-backup-1`. 4. Read both API answers and the framework's protocol log from the recorded position | Step 2: HTTP 422 with error code `validation_failed`. Step 3: the gateway's standard `capability_unavailable` answer, not HTTP 422. Neither request produces an incoming `backups.delete` frame in the framework's protocol log: the normal identifier is refused at the gateway's capability gate, not forwarded for a framework answer | server | |
| ultitools.upm.install.sha256.neg-long-changelog | Env: `staging`; `language: en` in config.yml; console access to the staging server running the handed-over build; the handover names `<long-module>` and `<long-version>`, a public installable catalogue version whose changelog has exactly 53,983 characters — either an unchanged migrated changelog or, when staging holds no migrated data, a changelog set to that text directly in the staging database (creating a version caps a changelog at 5,000 characters) — and provides the expected changelog text; no version of that module is installed; the modules folder initially has no matching JAR or `.part` file | 1. Read the level-filtered catalogue version page and the single-version response for the named fixture; compare both changelogs with the handover text and record the single version's `sha256` as `<H>` and `sizeBytes` as `<S>`. 2. Before installation, inspect the handed-over head's `VerifiedDownload#verdictOfCompleted` and `PluginInstallUtils#downloadAndAdmit`: size and SHA-256 verification must succeed before `moveIntoPlace`. 3. Run `/upm versions <long-module>`, then `/upm install <long-module> <long-version>`. 4. Compute the installed JAR's SHA-256 and byte size; inspect the modules folder | Both catalogue reads preserve all 53,983 changelog characters exactly, and the version list contains `<long-version>`. Installation succeeds with `Installed! Please restart the server! Please be sure to delete the old version module!`; the final `<long-module>-<long-version>.jar` matches `<H>` and `<S>` and no matching `.part` remains. The observed installed bytes match the metadata recorded before installation, and the source check confirms that both integrity checks precede admission; an unreadable/oversized response, truncated text or any mismatch is `fail` | server | |
| ultitools.upm.versions.neg-first-page-only | Env: `staging`; `language: en` in config.yml; console access to the staging server running the handed-over build; the coordinator has prepared `<paged-module>` with at least six public versions installable at this framework's API level and recorded their catalogue order; exactly one filtered version has `isLatest: true`; the catalogue is not being published to during this row; no version of this module is installed | 1. Read `/v1/catalogue/modules/<paged-module>/versions?apiLevel=L&minApiLevel=700&page=N&pageSize=5` for every page, starting at `N=1`; record the first page's `total`, every version name in order and the `isLatest` version. 2. Run `/upm versions <paged-module>` and compare every numbered entry. 3. Run `/upm install <paged-module>` without a version argument. 4. Inspect the final JAR filename and its `plugin.yml` version | The command lists exactly the first page's `total` versions, with every name in catalogue order, including entries after the first five. The installed filename and `plugin.yml` version equal the filtered catalogue's `isLatest` version. A truncated list, duplicate/omitted version or different installed version is `fail` | server | |

Open the panel's server-properties editor and wait for its successful read; record the file's SHA-256 and the observer position. 2. Leave every property unchanged, focus and blur a field without editing it, and attempt Save in the editor. 3. Inspect the panel's network recording and the framework protocol log for this interaction; compute the file's SHA-256 again | The successful initial properties read is the positive control. The unchanged edit sends no properties-update HTTP request and no `properties.update` frame reaches the framework; the file's SHA-256 remains unchanged. An empty `changes` update sent by the panel, any forwarded update frame or a file change is `fail`; a disabled Save control does not authorize bypassing the UI to inject a request | server | |

## 6.5.0 supplemental acceptance probes

Retired for 7.0.0: `ultitools.i18n.declared-missing.warns-once`,
`ultitools.i18n.declared-missing.alias-merged`,
`ultitools.i18n.declared-missing.disk-copy-does-not-mask`,
`ultitools.i18n.complete-declaration.silent`. The two legacy declaration forms and their missing-resource check were removed.

These rows preserve the executable acceptance obligations of the 6.5.0 fixes. Use disposable probe
modules compiled against the framework build under test (Java 25 bytecode for the 7.0 baseline, a supported module
`api-version` no higher than the framework's API level). Install modules in
`plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`, not Bukkit's `plugins/`. Probe source is part of the test handover;
if a prerequisite probe cannot load for reasons other than the assertion under test, record blocked,
not a vacuous pass. All message counts below are scoped to the indicated start or request window.
Each stub request uses a fresh id; observe replies by replyTo, never just the newest frame.

- **Cache probes:** UatCacheBad has `AbstractBadHolder` in `com.ultikits.uat.cachebad`, declaring
  `@PlayerCache(saveBeforeRemove = true) protected final Map<UUID, String> data = new HashMap<>();`.
  Its concrete singleton `BadCacheHolder` does not implement PlayerCacheSaver. UatCacheGood has the
  same inherited field on AbstractGoodHolder and a singleton GoodCacheHolder implementing
  PlayerCacheSaver. Its callback verifies the UUID is still in data, then logs INFO
  `uatcachegood saved <uuid>`; a violated check throws. Player `/uatcachegood put` inserts the player's
  UUID and replies `uatcachegood stored`; console `/uatcachegood size` replies `uatcachegood size <n>`.
  Both are standard UltiTools modules with those module names and lowercase identify-strings.
- **Error probes:** UatErrA (identify-string uaterra) exposes console `/uaterra boom <text>`, throwing
  IllegalStateException with `uaterr boom <text>`; `/uaterra bean` calls an @ExceptionCatch method on
  a @Service bean throwing IllegalArgumentException with `uaterr bean`; `/uaterra long` throws
  IllegalStateException with 499 `a` characters followed by three supplementary emoji and `tail`.
  UatErrB (uaterrb) copies the boom handler's method names and line numbers, differing only in
  package/command/module names; install it as `Uat Err B-1.0.0.jar`. UatErrNoId has no identify-string,
  module name UatErrNoId, and `/uaterrnoid boom x` throws `uaterr boom x`. Plain Bukkit plugin
  UatPlain version 1.0.0, installed in Bukkit's plugins directory, has `/uatplain boom` throwing
  IllegalStateException with `uatplain boom`. Use the English server and enabled error reporting;
  wrapper reports do not replace the specifically named report. Read stub validation logs as well
  as captured frames; the absence of a report is never a pass.

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.playercache.save-without-saver.refused | Env: `server`; `language: en`; the framework build under test; the disposable server and probe fixtures described above; both cache probes installed; fresh server start | 1. Start and wait for `Done (`. 2. Read the startup console and `logs/latest.log`. 3. Run console `/list` and `/uatcachegood size`. | Step 2: an error names UatCacheBad and contains `@PlayerCache field 'data'`, `declared on com.ultikits.uat.cachebad.AbstractBadHolder`, `requests saveBeforeRemove = true`, `bean com.ultikits.uat.cachebad.BadCacheHolder` and `does not implement PlayerCacheSaver`. The framework's module-loading result reports UatCacheBad failed, with no successful module-loading result for it. UatCacheGood is not named in an error. Step 3: `/list` answers and `uatcachegood size 0`. Do not infer whether the bad module's own enable code ran before refusal. (Source: PlayerCacheManager#registerBean) | server | |

P1 runs `/uatcachegood put`. 2. Console runs `/uatcachegood size`. 3. P1 quits. 4. After 5 seconds run console `/uatcachegood size` and read logs since step 3. | Step 1: `uatcachegood stored`.
